# Label review — 40 questions

For each question, tick `[x]` on **every** passage that contains the answer (compare with the book answer shown).

- Pre-ticked boxes are the harness's **guesses** (keyword/number overlap with the book answer). Please check and correct them.
- Passages marked **outside vector top-10** were found by scanning the whole book. Ticking one means the answer exists but vector search missed it.
- If no passage contains the answer, tick `NONE`.
- To add a passage that isn't listed, put its chunk_id after `ADD:` (comma-separated).

When finished, run: `python -m eval.build_labels apply`

## bsava-002 · contraindication

**Q:** When should acepromazine be avoided?  
**Book answer:** In hypotension due to shock, trauma or cardiovascular disease, in animals under 3 months old and in animals with liver disease. Large breeds are very sensitive when dosed on a mg/kg basis, so use the low end of the range. In Boxers, use low doses (0.01 mg/kg i.m.) or avoid it, because fainting and syncope from sinoatrial block can occur.

## bsava-003 · dose

**Q:** What is the dose of amlodipine for systemic hypertension in cats?  
**Book answer:** 0.625-1.25 mg per cat p.o. q24h. The dose may be increased slowly or the frequency raised to q12h if needed, and blood pressure monitoring is essential. It appears safe even with concurrent renal failure.

## bsava-007 · concept

**Q:** Is apomorphine recommended to induce emesis in cats?  
**Book answer:** No. It is not recommended in cats; the formulary notes that xylazine is a potent emetic in cats and at least as safe.

## bsava-014 · dose

**Q:** What is the dose of buprenorphine in dogs and cats?  
**Book answer:** 20 µg/kg i.v., i.m. or s.c. q6h for both species. In cats it is also well tolerated and effective given sublingually.

## bsava-022 · dose

**Q:** What is the dose of cefovecin (Convenia) and how often can it be repeated?  
**Book answer:** 8 mg/kg s.c., equivalent to 1 ml per 10 kg of reconstituted drug. It may be repeated after 14 days, up to three times.

## bsava-023 · predisposition

**Q:** In which animals should cefovecin not be used?  
**Book answer:** Small herbivores such as rabbits and guinea pigs, and dogs and cats under 8 weeks old. Its use should also be avoided in pregnant and lactating animals.

## bsava-036 · dose

**Q:** What is the dose of diazepam for status epilepticus in dogs?  
**Book answer:** A bolus of 0.5-1 mg/kg i.v., or intrarectally if there is no venous access. Onset is 2-3 minutes i.v., so it can be repeated every 10 minutes up to three times if there is no effect.

## bsava-068 · concept

**Q:** How long a wash-out period is advised before switching from one NSAID to another?

## bsava-078 · concept

**Q:** Can paracetamol be used in cats?  
**Book answer:** No. Cats lack the glucuronyl transferase enzymes needed to metabolize it. The dog dose is 10 mg/kg p.o. q12h.

## bsava-083 · contraindication

**Q:** In which cardiac conditions is pimobendan contraindicated?  
**Book answer:** Hypertrophic cardiomyopathy, and conditions where cardiac output cannot be increased by stronger contraction, such as aortic stenosis.

**Book answer:** It can cause severe coughing due to eosinophilic bronchitis, which may be fatal.

## bw-001 · predisposition

**Q:** Why are cats more susceptible to acetaminophen toxicosis than dogs?  
**Book answer:** Cats cannot effectively glucuronidate acetaminophen because of a genetic deficiency in the glucuronide conjugation pathway. They can be poisoned by as little as 50-60 mg/kg (often half a tablet), and rapidly develop methemoglobinemia.

## bw-003 · dose

**Q:** What is the fomepizole dosing schedule for ethylene glycol toxicosis in dogs?  
**Book answer:** 20 mg/kg IV initially, then 15 mg/kg IV at 12 and 24 hours, then 5 mg/kg IV at 36 hours. The book describes fomepizole as an effective, non-toxic liver alcohol dehydrogenase inhibitor that needs less intensive care than ethanol.

## bw-007 · predisposition

**Q:** Which species is at risk of systemic lily poisoning, and what organ is affected?  
**Book answer:** Cats. Ingestion of Lilium or Hemerocallis (daylily) leaves or flowers causes a severe nephrotoxic syndrome, with as little as 2-3 leaves reported to be dangerous. Dogs only get mild GI upset.

## bw-013 · concept

**Q:** What pathologic findings are associated with NSAID toxicosis?  
**Book answer:** GI irritation, ulceration or hemorrhage with possible gastric perforation and peritonitis, plus renal tubular or papillary necrosis or interstitial nephritis. H2 blockers such as ranitidine or famotidine are used for GI ulceration.

## bw-019 · dose

**Q:** What is the initial insulin protocol for diabetic ketoacidosis?  
**Book answer:** Regular insulin is the insulin of choice, starting at 0.2 U/kg IM (or SC if hydration is normal), then 0.1-0.2 U/kg 3-6 hours later, which may be given hourly if needed.

## bw-022 · dose

**Q:** What is the oral methimazole dose for feline hyperthyroidism, and what is the most effective treatment?  
**Book answer:** Methimazole 5-15 mg/day divided q8-12h; it can also be given transdermally in PLO gel, though that is slower. Radioiodine therapy is described as arguably the safest and most effective treatment.

## bw-027 · concept

**Q:** What blood pressure values define systemic hypertension in dogs and cats?  
**Book answer:** Systolic above 160 mmHg or diastolic above 120 mmHg, measured by any method.

## bw-032 · concept

**Q:** What are the hallmark features of FIP?  
**Book answer:** Insidious onset, persistent fever that does not respond to treatment, pyogranulomatous tissue reaction, exudative effusions in body cavities and high mortality.

## bw-034 · dose

**Q:** What is the dose of zidovudine for FIV and what must be monitored?  
**Book answer:** 5-15 mg/kg PO q12h. It is most effective against acute infection, and bone marrow toxicity must be monitored.

## bw-037 · concept

**Q:** What are the antibiotic options for Lyme disease in dogs?  
**Book answer:** Doxycycline 5-10 mg/kg PO q12h with food, amoxicillin 20 mg/kg PO q8-12h, or azithromycin 25 mg/kg PO q24h. Doxycycline is preferred if Anaplasma phagocytophilum co-infection is present.

**Q:** What is the treatment of choice for canine ehrlichiosis?

## bw-041 · concept

**Q:** What is the recommended treatment once rabies is diagnosed?  
**Book answer:** There is no treatment. Once the diagnosis is certain, euthanasia is indicated, and suspected cases must be securely isolated.

## bw-044 · concept

**Q:** What clinical forms can feline calicivirus cause?  
**Book answer:** Upper respiratory disease, oral ulceration, pneumonia, occasionally arthritis, or a highly fatal systemic hemorrhagic disease.

## bw-049 · dose

**Q:** How are pancreatic enzymes dosed for EPI?  
**Book answer:** Powdered enzymes mixed into food at 1 teaspoon per 10 kg body weight with each meal, with at least two meals a day. Pre-incubating the enzymes with food does not improve their effect.

## bw-050 · predisposition

**Q:** What is GDV and which dogs are at risk?  
**Book answer:** A condition where the stomach dilates and then rotates around its short axis. Large, deep-chested breeds such as Great Danes and German shepherds are at risk, and risk increases with age.

## bw-057 · dose

**Q:** How is prerenal azotemia distinguished from acute kidney injury?  
**Book answer:** Prerenal azotemia has concentrated urine (USG at least 1.030 in dogs and 1.035 in cats) and is correctable with fluid repletion.

## bw-094 · contraindication

**Q:** Why should the prolapsed third eyelid gland be replaced rather than removed?  
**Book answer:** Because the gland produces up to 50% of the aqueous tear film, and excising it predisposes to KCS. Surgical replacement (imbrication technique) is recommended.

## bw-100 · predisposition

**Q:** Which animals are predisposed to heat stroke?  
**Book answer:** Longhaired and brachycephalic animals, very young or old animals, young dogs that overexert, and old dogs with preexisting disease. Cats are uncommonly affected.
